# What the VPC/SP exchange counters add up to, window by window, over the dumps already in git
#       (Resident Evil 4, re4.exe, D3D12 via vkd3d-proton 3.1.0, 2560x1440. The arms differ in
#       render mode, cache mode, clock and frame rate - never in the build, so no arm changes
#       the number of vertex-shader outputs; `results/games/README.md` is the registry of arms).
# regenerate: scripts/vpc-sp-pair.py --dumps 'results/games/**/*.txt' \
#                 > results/games/re4/vpc-sp-pair.txt
# reads: committed dumps, found by their own header rather than by file name. The corpus is the
#       game runs, because the question is about the game; the count, the per-title breakdown and
#       how many dumps carry the pair are in the `# scanned` and `# carries both` blocks below,
#       which are derived - do not quote a count from prose. The default glob also picks up
#       results/synthetics/, whose probe runs carry neither selector: journal 40.36 cites that run
#       separately, for the two PC columns. Every window goes through scripts/fdperf_sections.py.
#       Nothing here samples the GPU: the script reads files. A dump under a gitignored raw/
#       directory is skipped - the pre-curation copy of a run, which no claim cites (AGENTS.md).
# ids: the value= attributes of A7XX_PERF_<GROUP>_<name> in
#       ../mesa27-src/src/freedreno/registers/adreno/a7xx_perfcntrs.xml, so VPC 10 is
#       A7XX_PERF_VPC_SP_COMPONENTS, SP 48 is A7XX_PERF_SP_EXPORT_VPC_TRANS, PC 19 is
#       A7XX_PERF_PC_VERTEX_HITS and PC 39 is A7XX_PERF_PC_TAG_CHECKED_VERTICES. The XML
#       gives no desc= attribute for any of them, so no column below is a decoded quantity
#       (journal 40.7, 40.11, 40.33): each is a ratio of two counters. Queue 34's
#       "PC_TAG_CHECKED_VERTICES (id 19)" conflates the last two ids.
# re-cuts: journal 40.34's row for this pair called the two counters one signal from a ratio
#       of per-arm medians (1.00000) over the windows of t_rel 20-105 s at CP_ALWAYS_COUNT
#       >= 676 MHz (1176 of 1293 windows in 33 arms, results/games/re4/vpc-state.txt). This file
#       prints every window of every arm that carries the pair, the two raw deltas and their
#       difference as an integer, so "equal" is read as equality and not as a quotient inside a
#       tolerance. That clock filter is why vpc-state lists 33 arms and not 34: vpc-hp40 has no
#       window at >= 676 MHz, and its one all-idle window is where both selectors read exactly 0.
# values are window deltas from `fdperf -r 1000 -d` (docs/fdperf-a7xx.md, section Windows). The
#       `*M/s` columns are delta/1e6 and are millions of counts per second only while `clk MHz`
#       says the window really was one second of the pinned clock; `clk MHz` is that window's own
#       CP_ALWAYS_COUNT/1e6.
# MISSING means no cfg in tools/fdperf/ programmed that selector, so it is an unanswered question
#       and never a zero. DROPPED means this window's delta was thrown away as a u64 wraparound
#       or [invalid] by scripts/fdperf_sections.py and is kept out of every statistic below.
# Scope: every fdperf dump committed under the scanned pattern, read offline; nothing here
# samples the GPU. One dump is one `fdperf -r 1000 -d` session of one arm of one game run.
# scanned 72 dumps in 72 directories under 'results/games/**/*.txt'; 32 more dumps sit under a
#    gitignored raw/ directory and were not read: they are the pre-curation copies of the same runs
#    and no claim cites them
#   under results/games/re4: 72 dump directories, 72 dumps
# carries both id 10 and id 48: 34 arms - dbg-file, dbg-gmem, dbg-perf, dbg-sysmem, gmem-nobin,
#    sc-default, sc-forcebin, sc-gmem, sc-sysmem, sh-c0, sh-c1, sh-q0, sh-q3, tse-algo-bandwidth,
#    tse-algo-prefer-gmem, tse-forcecb, tse-tse1, tse2-cacheon, tse2-tse2, tse3-cbbase,
#    tse3-cbforce, tse3-tse3cb2, tse3-tse3cb3, vpc-cacheon, vpc-cb1, vpc-fl111, vpc-fl121, vpc-hp40,
#    vpc-hp50, vpc-hpbase2, vpc-lrz2, vpc-nopref, vpc-vpcbase1, vpc-vpcbase2
# carries exactly one of the two: 16 arms - bv-bvbase, bv-bvforce, bv-bvforce2, bv-bvforce3,
#    bv-bvforce4, bv-bvforce5, cb21-c0, cb21-c1, cb21-h0, cb21-h1, vpc2-vpc2b, vpc3-attrbuf,
#    vpc3-vpc3base, vr4-c0-base, vr4-c0-nofp16, vr4-c1-base
# carries neither: 22 arms - 0014ab-a, 0014ab-b, 0014ab-ship, lrz-base, lrz-base2, lrz-nocons,
#    lrz-nolrz, lrz-nolrz2, lrz-nolrzfc, lrz-nolrzfc2, lrz-nolz, lrz-nolzfc, mesa27-vertex, mesa27,
#    tr-base, tr-lrzfc-off, tr-lrzfc-on, tr-nocomp-perf, tr-nocomp, tr-noupload, tr-noupload2,
#    vr5-a-aliart
# dumps read: 72, samples read: 3767

# Every window of every arm that carries both selectors. `id10`/`id48` are raw window
# deltas; `diff %` is (id10-id48)/id10 in percent; the volume columns are the same deltas
# in millions of counts; `clk MHz` is CP_ALWAYS_COUNT/1e6, i.e. the window's own length at
# the pinned 680 MHz. MISSING = the cfg never programmed that selector; DROPPED = this
# window's value was thrown away as a wrapped or [invalid] delta.
arm                    win t_rel           id10           id48    id10-id48   diff %    attrM    byteM    vertM  vpcramM    workM    busyM  clk MHz id10/vert id48/vert

Same binary, same shaders, same session, so only the pixel cost and the render mode move:
  spbusy - windows split at the median SP_BUSY_CYCLES / CP_ALWAYS_COUNT of the arm, i.e. the
           half that spends a larger share of the core on the pixel side; `key` columns give the
           median quotient of each half, so the size of the pixel-side move is visible. The
           quotient sits near 10.4 because the SP domain is 12-wide (docs/howto-measure.md
           quotes the SP divider /12); only the relative split is used here.
  tiles  - windows with VSC_INPUT_TILES > 0 against windows with 0, inside one session; the
           `key` columns are the median tile count of each half

  spbusy: id10/vert then differs between the halves by 0.46 % at that median and 1.4 % at most, in vpc-hp40.

  VSC_INPUT_TILES is the binning test the repository already uses: `> 0` means a binning pass
  ran (tools/fdperf/fdperf.cfg-bv line 44 says the flag's run 'never started a binning pass
  (VSC_INPUT_TILES 0 in every window)'). So only an arm whose upper half is a real tile rate is
  a render-mode contrast:
    dbg-file               bins 53,734,558 tiles in the upper half against 0 in the lower, id10/vert 0.6827x
    not a mode contrast (the upper half is a handful of tiles): dbg-perf 13, sc-default 2, sh-q3 8, tse3-cbbase 528
  The spbusy split moves the pixel side by well under one percent, so on its own it only shows
  that id10/vert does not chase small pixel-load wiggles. The render-mode split above, where an
  arm really changes between binned and direct windows, is the one that moves it.

Render mode, not arm. VSC_INPUT_TILES is the repository's own test for a binning pass:
  tools/fdperf/fdperf.cfg-bv line 44 calls a run whose VSC_INPUT_TILES is 0 in every window one
  that 'never started a binning pass', so a window is read the same way here. The counter's own
  scale cannot be compared between dumps: the same selector 4 of the VSC group reads 5.4e7 per
  second in dbg-file and 1.5e3 in tse3-cbforce, one title, one resolution, one frame. Only the
  gap inside the data is used to class a window, and a rate a 2560x1440 frame could not produce
  is named for what it is instead of being read as a tile count:
  binned     :  146 windows, the counter between 3,258,344 and 55,713,162  <- a rate only a binning pass at frame rate produces
  low rate   :  227 windows, the counter between 1 and 28,306  <- a non-zero rate no 2560x1440 frame can produce as bins
  zero       :  586 windows, the counter between 0 and 0  <- the test the repository uses for 'no binning pass started'
  value dropped:    1 windows, no VSC reading to class  <- this window's VSC delta was a u64 wraparound
  not sampled:  862 windows, no VSC reading to class  <- the cfg carried no VSC selector

  So the per-vertex reading of id 10 is not a property of the shader interface. Where one session
  contains both render modes, and the binary and both shaders cannot have changed, the counter
  per vertex moves by the factor printed above while the interface quotients do not move.

Windows read: 1824 over 34 arms (the table above).
No unit is derived here. The XML gives none (40.7, 40.11, 40.33), and no arm in git changes the
number of vertex-shader outputs, so queue 34's known-interface probe stays the only measurement
that can name one.
